Closes the gap surfaced at the TARROW-1 cycle-opening (Patch 0659): a finished, self-contained review *package* is not actionable until it is turned into **paste-ready, reviewer-addressed dispatch text** (Thomas did not know the artifact/file name and had no per-reviewer prompt to paste). On the command **"initiate review protocol"** (variants "execute review protocol" / "review protocol" / "dispatch review" / "send it to the reviewers") the assistant produces the **Dispatch Output**: (a) an identification header (programme = CPP + artifact ID + human title + version + patch + one-line what-it-is — so Thomas is never lost about *what* is under review); (b) the package's raw GitHub URL; (c) a **paste-ready dispatch prompt per active reviewer** (ChatGPT/Grok/Copilot default, optional Sonnet) using the reusable skeleton in protocol §4, with the reviewer steer drawn from the package's own §6; (d) a delivery-mode note (raw URL primary; "paste it" → full inline package body as zero-dependency fallback). Precondition codified: the review package must be **self-contained with the verify code EMBEDDED inline** (the Patch-0656 process-learning — a reviewer cannot reach the SCRIPT-EXECUTED tier from a filename reference). Proactive-offer rule: the assistant offers the protocol right after registering, single-pass, any artifact that answers a registered open problem or advances a registered verdict. **operating_system.md**: §1 gains the canonical "initiate review protocol" command paragraph alongside "execute handover protocol"; §5 gains a "Review dispatch" cross-ref subsection; the header changelog records the Patch-0660 clause. **Anti-priorities**: no physics/theorem/axiom change; the existing OS §5 review-cycle content (roles, tiers, lessons) is unchanged — this adds the dispatch *mechanics* on top. **Next (Session 150):** integrate the TARROW-1 reviews when they return (→ `reviews-CHIR-TARROW.md`; verdict-flip on Q4/Q1 → v1.1, else calibration + close 3/3); the new command is available for that and all future cycles.)

## Purpose

The axiom-registry tracks what CPP *assumes*. This file tracks what CPP has *proved*. Together they define the deductive structure of the theory. The primary health metric is the ratio:

**Theorems : Axioms = 67 : 9 ≈ 7.44 theorems per axiom**

*(Counting theorems + corollaries; PROP-SF-4-2 counted separately as proposition; LEMMA-SF-4-1 counted separately as lemma. Axiom count uses the 6-axiom set from postulates_and_theorems.md. SF-4 v4.3 (the current SHIP-ready state) registers: THEO-SF-4-1 (K3-Cage-Shell Consistency; clauses (i)+(ii) exact, clause (iii) at conditional theorem closure level via THEO-SF-4-5 as of v4.0; previously conditional on SM-5 antibonding-doublet TBM-direction ansatz at v1.0), PROP-SF-4-2 ($\mu\tau$-exchange symmetry, exact), THEO-SF-4-3 (exact TBM angle recovery, tautological-by-construction), THEO-SF-4-4 ($\alpha$-Exponent Reduction at the Bound/Unbound Boundary, conditional theorem closure at v3.0; resolves OPEN-FP-SF-4-1), LEMMA-SF-4-1 (No-Anchor Correlator Suppression, supporting lemma for THEO-SF-4-4 sub-claim (c), added at v4.1 in response to ChatGPT v4.0 review), THEO-SF-4-5 (Composite K3-Cage-Shell Coupling Theorem, conditional theorem closure at v4.0; resolves OPEN-FP-SF-4-2 and SM-5 op:nu_id cross-sector — first cross-sector closure in CPP). All v4.x closures are conditional theorem closures within the current CPP theorem stack (see rem:conditional_closure in SF-4 v4.2+); the conditional-closure framework is documented programme-wide in templates/conditional_closure_framework.md (added at patch 0340). SS-8 v1.0 added THEO-SS-13, THEO-SS-14, THEO-SS-15 — note that THEO-SS-14 and THEO-SS-15 are conditional theorems on paper-level structural hypotheses C1–C4 + D1–D3, not unconditional from the programme axiom set; per OS §11.7 these are still counted as theorems for the registry totals. # Strong Sector (SS) — 15 Theorems, 1 Proposition

Sources: SS-1 (Strong Sector unified paper), SS-3 (SU(3) uniqueness), SS-7 (alpha-cluster regime), SS-8 (interstitial-neutron 2E/V scaling), SS-9 (simplicial alpha-polytope connectivity, conditional theorem closure paper).

| ID | Name | Result | Axioms Used | Paper Reference |
|----|------|--------|-------------|-----------------|
| **THEO-SS-1** | SU(3) from tetrahedral hopping | The three colour-charge hopping operators on the K₃ base graph generate the su(3) Lie algebra exactly. T^a = λ^a/2 (Gell-Mann matrices) reproduced to machine precision. | AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-4 | SS-1, Theorem 1 |
| **THEO-SS-2** | Gluon masslessness | Open hDP chain paths have f_geom = 0; gluons are massless. Same topological argument as photon masslessness. | AXIM-2, AXIM-3, AXIM-4 | SS-1, Theorem 2 |
| **THEO-SS-3** | β₀ = 7 | One-loop QCD β-function coefficient: β₀ = 11C_A/3 − 4T_F n_f/3 = 11−4 = 7. From C_A = 3, T_F = 1/2 (both exact), n_f = 6 (cage architecture). | AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-4 | SS-1, Theorem 3 |
| **THEO-SS-4** | α_geom exact | α_geom = 3(11+5√5)√(5+√5)/320 ≈ 0.5594. Pure 600-cell Voronoi invariant, closed form. Same constant appears in SR coupling and EM stiffness. | AXIM-2 | SS-1, Theorem 4 |
| **THEO-SS-5** | k_SM derived | k_SM = α_geom/(12φ²) ≈ 0.01781. Per-vertex dimensionless coupling from Voronoi stiffness. | AXIM-2 | SS-1, Theorem 5 |
| **THEO-SS-6** | sea_strength derived | sea_strength = (N_lattice/z) × k_SM = 10 × k_SM ≈ 0.1780. Factor 10 = 120/12, exact geometric integer. | AXIM-2 | SS-1, Theorem 6 |
| **THEO-SS-7** | GMO mass formula | Gell-Mann–Okubo baryon mass relation from SU(3) Casimirs. Octet relation holds to 0.6%. | AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-4 | SS-1, Theorem 7 |
| **THEO-SS-8** | Decuplet equal spacing | Equal mass spacing in baryon decuplet. Ω⁻ prediction: 1681 MeV vs PDG 1672.5 MeV (0.5%). | AXIM-1, AXIM-2 | SS-1, Theorem 8 |
| **THEO-SS-9** | Quark mass strict ordering | m_u < m_d < m_s < m_c < m_b < m_t from cage depth: each shell adds positive binding energy. Direction m_u < m_d from SSV polarity asymmetry. | AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-5 | SS-1, Theorem 9 |
| **THEO-SS-10** | SU(3) uniqueness | SU(3) is the unique Lie algebra of the tetrahedral cage. Any 8 linearly independent traceless Hermitian 3×3 operators generate su(3) and nothing else. Resolves OPEN-SS-11. | AXIM-1, AXIM-2 | SS-3, Theorem 3.3 |
| **PROP-SS-11** | Physical basis transformation | The 4+4 physical mode basis {L₁...L₄, H₁...H₄} spans su(3). Change-of-basis matrix M has det = 2/√3. Inverse: T³ = ½(L₂−L₄), T⁸ = (√3/2)(L₂+L₄). | AXIM-1, AXIM-2 | SS-3, Proposition 6.5 |
| **THEO-SS-12** | Simplicial polytope edge count | For a simplicial polytope on $N_\alpha \geq 4$ vertices (closed triangulated 2-sphere), Euler's formula $V - E + F = 2$ combined with the triangle constraint $2E = 3F$ give $E = 3N_\alpha - 6$ exactly. Independent of which specific polytope is realised; the octahedron and triangular antiprism at $N_\alpha = 6$ both have 12 edges. Basis of the SS-7 $3N_\alpha - 6$ binding-edge formula. | Pure graph theory (Euler's formula); invoked in SS-7 under structural assumption C4 (alpha clusters realise simplicial polytope connectivity) — not yet derived from CPP axioms, OPEN-SS-24 targets that derivation | SS-7 v1.2, Theorem 2.1 |
| **THEO-SS-13** | Euler-degree theorem | For a simplicial 3-polytope with $V$ vertices, the average vertex degree is $2E/V = 6 - 12/V$ exactly, where $E = 3V - 6$ is the edge count from Euler's formula. Combinatorial corollary of THEO-SS-12 specialized to vertex-degree analysis. Provides the geometric basis for the SS-8 interstitial-neutron 2E/V scaling law. | Pure graph theory (corollary of THEO-SS-12) | SS-8 v1.0, Theorem `thm:euler-degree` |
| **THEO-SS-14** | D1 — Conditional vertex localization (Level-1+2 independence) | An interstitial neutron added to a bulk simplicial alpha-polytope localizes at an alpha-vertex (rather than at edge-midpoint, face-center, or centroid) under either of two functionally independent realizations of proximity-binding: Model A (K₃-edge counting under D2) and Model B (short-range Yukawa pair physics). Q2 algebraic reduction test confirms Model B does not reduce to Model A under any short-range regime (multiplicity vectors, non-vertex orderings, vertex-degree scaling all distinguish). Level-1 algebraic independence and Level-2 functional independence proved; Level-3 physical-principle independence open as OPEN-SS-26 PARTIAL (both models share a proximity-binding ancestor). | A1, A2, A11; SS-5 collective-mode framework; D2 (under Model A) | SS-8 v1.0, Theorem `thm:d1-cond` |
| **THEO-SS-15** | 2E/V interstitial scaling law (conditional) | For a simplicial alpha-polytope on $N_\alpha$ vertices in the bulk regime, the per-neutron interstitial binding strength is $\Delta_1(N_\alpha) = (2E/V) \cdot B_{\text{pair}} = (6 - 12/N_\alpha) \cdot B_{\text{pair}}$ where $B_{\text{pair}} = M_0/\varphi = 2.342$ MeV is the third-scale recurrence of the SS-5 K₃-mode quantum. Twelve concurrent zero-parameter empirical predictions across the strict $N{=}Z$ alpha-chain at $N_\alpha \in [3, 14]$, with two sub-1% agreements at the most symmetric polytopes (${}^{26}$Mg octahedron, ${}^{42}$Ca gyroelongated square bipyramid). Conditional on C1–C4 (inherited from SS-7) plus D1 (THEO-SS-14), D2, and D3. | A2, A5, A8', A11; THEO-SS-12, THEO-SS-13, THEO-SS-14; conditional on C1–C4 + D1–D3 | SS-8 v1.0, Theorem `thm:h2prime` |
| **THEO-SS-16** | Simplicial alpha-polytope connectivity (conditional theorem closure paper) | Five-clause conditional theorem deriving from the CPP lattice geometry under hypothesis stack C1$'$ + C2 + C3 + C5 + C6 + C7 + C8 + rigid packing + 3D-non-degeneracy: **(i)** the alpha-cluster contact graph $G(\mathcal{C})$ is connected; **(ii)** the alpha-cluster centroids are non-coplanar in $\mathbb{R}^3$; **(iii)** $|E(G)| = 3N_\alpha - 6$ exactly (equivalent to THEO-SS-12 specialized to the alpha-cluster regime); **(iv)** for $N_\alpha \in \{4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12\}$ (the FvdW values), $G$ is the 1-skeleton of the unique Freudenthal--van der Waerden convex deltahedron at $N_\alpha$, and the centroid configuration realizes that deltahedron geometrically with edges at uniform contact length $R_{\alpha\alpha}$; **(v)** $G$ is planar and triangulated. The proof routes through three lemmas (Lemma A pairwise triangular contact from C1$'$+C2; Lemma C energy minimization picks maximum edges from C3+C5; Lemma B$'$ contact graph $=$ 1-skeleton of convex 3-polytope from Lemma A + Lemma C + C6 + C7 + C8 via Euler's formula and Steinitz's theorem) plus three sub-lemmas (Sub-Lemma 2.1 conditional C7 derivation from C1$'$+C2+C6+(H4) cluster contractibility+(H5) alpha-surface adjacency; Sub-Lemma 2.2 3D-non-degeneracy from maximum-edge selection at FvdW values; Sub-Lemma 2.3 C5 well-definedness via compactness + USC at $N_\alpha \geq 2$). Refined-C1 facet (b) is a necessary precondition for C8's plausibility at $N_\alpha \geq 7$ where the FvdW deltahedron has degree-$\geq 5$ vertices that strict-C1 (each alpha presenting four outer faces) cannot host. The simplicial-polytope edge count $E = 3N_\alpha - 6$ used as a paper-level structural hypothesis (assumption C4) in SS-7 and inherited by SS-8 is now derived as a *theorem clause* from the more fundamental hypothesis stack — conditional on C5/C6/C7/C8 first-principles closure (registered as OPEN-SS-29/30/33/37 respectively). v1.0 SHIPPED 7 May 2026 Session 32 on the explicit basis of seven independent AI review passes (d.1–d.7) all converging on v1.0-ready; sub-task (e) external/human review rescoped from "blocking gate" to "open invitation post-v1.0 ship via public posting." | A1–A11 (CPP axiom set); conditional on C1$'$ (refined-C1, multi-faceted alpha rigidity, inherited from SS-7 v1.3), C2 (face-to-face contact at distance $R_{\alpha\alpha}$, inherited from SS-7), C3 (K₃ collective mode at each contact, inherited from SS-7), C5 (ground-state energy minimization, OPEN-SS-29 first-principles closure), C6 (cluster surface-realization, OPEN-SS-30), C7 (contact-graph planarity, OPEN-SS-33 ADVANCED via Sub-Lemma 2.1), C8 (FvdW centroid-realizability, OPEN-SS-37 with 4 candidate closure routes including Route (d) distance-geometry/EDM/rigidity), rigid packing, 3D-non-degeneracy (Sub-Lemma 2.2 closes unconditionally) | SS-9 v1.0, Theorem 6.1 (Simplicial Alpha-Polytope Connectivity); Sub-Lemmas 2.1, 2.2, 2.3 in §2.5–§2.7 |

# SF-Line Flagship Papers (SF) — 10 Theorems, 7 Propositions, 1 Lemma

Sources: SF-2 (electroweak cage-boson unification from 600-cell geometry, v1.0 SHIPPED Session 83 — joint main paper + Companion v1.0 SHIP with three-reviewer convergence on SHIP-at-v1.0 verdict); SF-4 (neutrino sector unification, v4.4 archival-deposit-quality Session 81 — conditional theorem closure paper with first cross-sector closure in CPP); **Capotauro sub-claim (c) v1.0 closure Session 102 Patch 0396** (Composite Capotauro Wigner-Eckart Theorem; chirality matrix element $|M| = \chi/6 = \phi^{-3}/6$ on K3-doublet; primary empirical prediction $\Delta p_{LR} \approx 0.0394$ validated within 2%; FI-C-10 cage-shell extension to chirality observables added as new foundational input in trajectory; Q11 sin²θ₁₃ derivation re-scoped to SF-2 v2.0+ work).

*Note: SF-line papers are flagship synthesis papers covering Sector Foundations questions. SF-4 was the first SF-line paper to ship at v1.0 (Session 54) and the first to reach conditional-theorem-closure SHIP-ready state at v4.3 (Session 77). SF-2 is the second SF-line paper to ship at v1.0 (Session 83 close, Patches 0345–0368 across Sessions 81–83) and the first to ship with an integrated Companion paper providing visual / pedagogical / exploratory-computational content alongside the main structural-derivation core. The cage-shell mass formula in SF-4 achieves conditional theorem closure at v3.0 via THEO-SF-4-4 (resolves OPEN-FP-SF-4-1). The K3-Cage-Shell Consistency Theorem (THEO-SF-4-1) closes all three clauses at v4.0 via the Composite K3-Cage-Shell Coupling Theorem (THEO-SF-4-5; resolves OPEN-FP-SF-4-2 and SM-5 op:nu_id cross-sector — first cross-sector closure in CPP). SF-2 delivers four cage-shape uniqueness theorems at theorem-level rigor (W bracelet, Z icosahedron, H dodecahedron, mass-gap) plus the W⁰ catalyst framework formalized as six propositions PROP-SF-2-1 through PROP-SF-2-6 plus Yang-Mills EFT continuum-limit recovery at proof-outline level (THEO-SF-2-5; Layer 4 dedicated future paper per PD-004). All SF-2 closures are conditional theorem closures within the current CPP theorem stack; the conditional-closure framework is documented programme-wide in `templates/conditional_closure_framework.md`.*

| ID | Name | Result | Axioms Used | Paper Reference |
|----|------|--------|-------------|-----------------|
| **THEO-SF-2-1** | W bracelet uniqueness | The W bracelet — a regular hexagonal closed 6-cycle in the 600-cell graph — is the unique cage geometry hosting the charged W gauge bosons. Among all 4800 induced 6-cycles in the 600-cell under $H_4$ action, exactly 1200 are regular hexagons (i.e., have $D_6$ stabilizer) and form a single orbit; the other 3600 have lower stabilizer. The 1200-orbit is the unique maximum-symmetry orbit of induced 6-cycles in the substrate. The $D_6$ stabilizer structurally selects the bracelet for catalyst-substrate role: the 6-vertex regular hexagonal ring has a $D_6$-symmetric centroid serving as the SSV-gradient minimum (Prop centroid) for external-charge capture (Prop capture). The bracelet's $D_6$-symmetric zero-net-charge structure (alternating $+$/$-$ charge per vertex) drives the substrate-symmetry expectation $\Pi_{33} - \Pi_{3Q} \approx 0$ for the $W^0$ contribution to the precision-electroweak oblique parameter $S$ (verified at parametric-scaling level by Companion sensitivity-scan §5.7). | AXIM-1, AXIM-4; topological invariants of 600-cell + $H_4$ action; orbit-stabilizer theorem | SF-2 v1.0, Theorem 4.2 |
| **THEO-SF-2-2** | Z icosahedral uniqueness | The Z boson cage geometry is uniquely the 12-vertex first Euclidean distance shell of the 600-cell at $d^2 = 1/\phi^2$, with $I_h$ icosahedral stabilizer. The 12-vertex first-shell icosahedron is the unique closed-cage geometry at this distance shell admitting full icosahedral symmetry. Combined with the dodecahedral duality realized geometrically (Cor icoduality), the Z and H cages occupy the two consecutive distance shells with mutually-dual stabilizers ($I_h$ each), giving the cage-boson family natural geometric placement in the 600-cell. | AXIM-1, AXIM-4; 600-cell distance-shell structure; icosahedral group $I_h$ classification | SF-2 v1.0, Theorem 4.1 |
| **THEO-SF-2-3** | H dodecahedral uniqueness | The Higgs boson cage geometry is uniquely the 20-vertex second Euclidean distance shell of the 600-cell at $d^2 = 1$, with $I_h$ icosahedral stabilizer realized via Platonic duality from the 12-vertex first-shell icosahedron. The 20-vertex second-shell dodecahedron is the unique closed-cage geometry at this distance shell. Combined with Cor higgs_scalar ($A_5 \to J=0$ for the Higgs): the dodecahedron's $A_5$ rotation group admits only the trivial odd-dimensional representation at finite-symmetry level, delivering scalar character for the Higgs at theorem-level finite-group rigor; relativistic Lorentz-scalar character inherits via the continuum-limit Yang-Mills EFT (THEO-SF-2-5). | AXIM-1, AXIM-4; 600-cell distance-shell structure; icosahedral-dodecahedral Platonic duality; $A_5$ rotation group representation classification | SF-2 v1.0, Theorem 4.3 |
| **THEO-SF-2-4** | Electroweak mass-gap prediction | No closed cage with vertex count $V$ in $\{13, 14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19\}$ admits the symmetry and binding-energy structure required to be an electroweak boson cage in the 600-cell substrate. Combined with THEO-SF-2-2 (Z = $V=12$) and THEO-SF-2-3 (H = $V=20$): the electroweak boson cage spectrum has a structural mass-gap between the icosahedral first shell and the dodecahedral second shell, with no intermediate-vertex cage admissible. Empirical prediction: no electroweak scalar exists below approximately 200 GeV (modulo the Higgs at 125 GeV) — falsifier if such a particle were observed. | AXIM-1, AXIM-4; 600-cell vertex-count distribution; binding-energy structure for cage-shape stability | SF-2 v1.0, Theorem 4.4 |
| **THEO-SF-2-5** | Yang-Mills EFT continuum limit (proof outline) | The continuum-limit effective field theory of CPP substrate dynamics on the 600-cell at the electroweak sector recovers $SU(2)_L \times U(1)_Y$ Yang-Mills with the correct gauge-coupling structure, Ward-identity-preserving renormalization, and one-loop running. The proof outline establishes: (i) $SU(2)_L$ algebra inherits from binary icosahedral group $\Gamma$ acting on 120 lattice vertices (THEO-SF-2-5a Nexus gauge invariance is a supporting structural result); (ii) edge-flow Nexus tick dynamics coarse-grain to abelian gauge connection in the long-wavelength limit; (iii) face-flow Nexus tick dynamics coarse-grain to non-abelian gauge connection. **Proof-outline-level only**; full continuum derivation (with explicit Lagrangian, gauge-fixing prescription, Ward-identity-preserving regulator, renormalization scheme matching, one-loop diagrammatic computation) is registered as Layer 4 dedicated future paper per PD-004. The proof outline is the strongest theorem-level statement achievable without writing the dedicated continuum-limit paper. | AXIM-1, AXIM-4, AXIM-7; binary icosahedral group $\Gamma$ structure (from SS-1); coarse-graining of substrate dynamics | SF-2 v1.0, Theorem 8.3 (proof outline) |

*Note: THEO-SD-1 through THEO-SD-10 were referenced in the SD series documentation suite but were not formally registered in `postulates_and_theorems.md`. Added to the registry 12 April 2026. THEO-SD-CHIR-N introduces a sub-prefix convention within the SD section paralleling the SF-Line section's THEO-CAP-N convention (Patch 0397): SD-CHIR-N theorems are cross-sector unification closures under the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella across the umbrella's five observable manifestations (mass-mixing chirality, electroweak V-A, electromagnetic handedness, thermodynamic causal arrow, cosmological-vacuum asymmetry); the umbrella's eventual closure pattern is substrate-level Layer 2 unification + N sector-specific Layer 3 closures for N observable manifestations. THEO-SD-CHIR-1 is the first such closure, covering the K3-doublet ↔ W-bracelet pair (manifestations (i)+(ii) of the umbrella's five-manifestation list). THEO-SD-CHIR-2 is the second such closure, extending to the qDP/eDP electromagnetic-handedness sector (manifestation (iii)); the three-way cross-sector unification across manifestations (i)+(ii)+(iii) at identical numerical magnitude $\chi/6$ confirms the umbrella's three-step closure pattern (substrate-locality + cage-shell factor + pairing-convention) as structurally complete across manifestations with structurally distinct $\zeta$ generators but identical numerical content — the umbrella's **unification-at-magnitude-level claim**, distinct from and stronger than its unification-at-mechanism-level claim. Manifestations (iv) thermodynamic causal arrow and (v) cosmological-vacuum asymmetry remain open for future closure under the same three-step pattern with sector-specific $\zeta$ generators.*

| **THEO-DSL-4** | $\mathcal{O}(\delta^k)$ Substrate-Current Parallel-to-$\hat{n}$ Structural Theorem at All Orders $k \geq 1$ (**fourth F-line flagship theorem under THEO-DSL-N sub-prefix convention**; publication-grade Layer 3 **unconditional** for the structural sub-question; **first Route C sequence 1 artifact** under the OPEN-FP-F1-1 trajectory; **closes the structural sub-question of OPEN-FP-F1-1** at all orders $k \geq 1$ via symmetry-only argument; coefficient $\alpha_k$ at $k \geq 2$ remains OPEN as target of Route C sequence 2) | Under (H1) vertex-aligned Reading C with $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ + (H2) 600-cell unit-vertex normalisation + (H3) icosahedral residual symmetry $H_3 = I_h$ at the host vertex (per THEO-DSL-2 + Patch 0571) + (H4) F.1 Theorem 6.1 perturbation-locality propagation (shell-locality of $\vec{j}_{DI}^{\text{net}}(v_{\text{host}})|_{\mathcal{O}(\delta^k)}$ to edges in $E_k(v_{\text{host}})$ at every $k \geq 1$ per THEO-DSL-1) + (H5) Mechanism A path-integral extension at $\mathcal{O}(\delta^k)$ at sketch level per OPEN-FP-F1-1 DG-1 sub-option (A), the $\mathcal{O}(\delta^k)$ coefficient $\vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}}) := \vec{j}_{DI}^{\text{net}}(v_{\text{host}})|_{\mathcal{O}(\delta^k)}$ of the substrate current at the host vertex satisfies $\vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}}) \parallel \hat{n}$ for every $k \geq 1$. In particular, $\vec{j}_2(v_{\text{host}}) \parallel \hat{n}$ at $k = 2$, closing the structural sub-question of OPEN-FP-F1-1 at publication-grade Layer 3 unconditional rigor. The full formal $\delta$-power series of the substrate current at the host vertex is therefore parallel to $\hat{n}$ at every non-zero order: $\vec{j}_{DI}^{\text{net}}(v_{\text{host}}; \delta) = (\sum_{k \geq 1} \alpha_k \delta^k)\,\hat{n}$, with the $k = 1$ coefficient $\alpha_1 = 6 r_0 / \phi^2$ already computed in F.1 v1.0 Theorem 7.1 per THEO-DSL-3. **Proof architecture in two lemmas + main proof** (Patch 0585 hardened artifact; 334 lines LaTeX; 9-page PDF; zero substantive warnings clean three-pass compile): **Lemma 1** (Order-by-order $I_h$-invariance of $\vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}})$): for every $g \in I_h$ fixing $v_{\text{host}}$ and every $k \geq 0$, $g \cdot \vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}}) = \vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}})$ — established via three steps: (i) $I_h$-equivariance of Mechanism A's rate function $r(\hat{e}) = r_0(1 + \delta\,\hat{e}\cdot\hat{n})$ under stabilization of $\hat{n}$ (since $g^{-1}\hat{n} = \hat{n}$ for $g \in I_h$ fixing $v_{\text{host}} = \hat{n}$), so $r(g\hat{e}) = r(\hat{e})$; (ii) $I_h$-permutation of the path set $\mathcal{P}_k(v_{\text{host}})$ via the graph-automorphism action inherited from the $\Hfour$-stabilizer of $v_{\text{host}}$, with (H4)'s shell-locality ensuring $g \in I_h$ stabilises $E_k(v_{\text{host}})$ setwise; (iii) $O(4)$-equivariance of edge-direction vectors $\vec{d}(g \cdot P) = g \cdot \vec{d}(P)$. Re-indexing the sum over paths by the $I_h$-action yields $g \cdot \vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}}) = \vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}})$. **Lemma 2** ($I_h$-invariant vectors in $\mathbb{R}^4$ at $v_{\text{host}}$): the subspace $\{\vec{w} \in \mathbb{R}^4 : g\cdot\vec{w} = \vec{w}\;\forall g \in I_h\} = \mathbb{R}\hat{n}$ — established via the central inversion element $\iota \in I_h$ (the order-2 element $\iota = -I_3$ acting on the 3D tangent space, with $I_h = I \times \{e, \iota\}$). On the orthogonal decomposition $\mathbb{R}^4 = \mathbb{R}\hat{n} \oplus T_{v_{\text{host}}} S^3$, the inversion $\iota$ acts as $\text{diag}(1, -1, -1, -1)$, forcing any $I_h$-fixed $\vec{w} = a\hat{n} + \vec{w}_\perp$ to satisfy $\vec{w}_\perp = -\vec{w}_\perp$, hence $\vec{w}_\perp = \vec{0}$ and $\vec{w} \in \mathbb{R}\hat{n}$. **Schur's lemma alternative** registered as Remark 3.6 of the hardened artifact: the 3D standard representation of $I_h$ on $T_{v_{\text{host}}} S^3$ is irreducible (one of the two 3D irreducible representations $T_{1g}$ or $T_{1u}$ of $I_h$), so by Schur's lemma the trivial 1-dimensional representation occurs in $\mathbb{R}^4$ with multiplicity 1 at the $\mathbb{R}\hat{n}$ summand. **Main proof assembles Lemmas 1+2**: Lemma 1 forces $\vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}})$ into the $I_h$-invariant subspace; Lemma 2 identifies that subspace as $\mathbb{R}\hat{n}$; conclusion $\vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}}) \in \mathbb{R}\hat{n}$ i.e. parallel to $\hat{n}$. **All-orders strengthening** (Corollary 4.2 of the hardened artifact): the hypotheses make no use of $k = 2$ specifically; the parallel-to-$\hat{n}$ conclusion holds at every $k \geq 1$ at no additional cost — a structural strengthening of the OPEN-FP-F1-1 closure target. Any future Route A or Route C-sequence-2 coefficient computation may therefore impose the ansatz $\vec{j}_k(v_{\text{host}}) = \alpha_k\hat{n}$ from the outset, reducing the computational target from a 4-vector to a scalar $\alpha_k$. **Consistency with F.1 v1.0 Theorem 7.1 at first order**: $k = 1$ specialisation reduces to $\vec{j}_1(v_{\text{host}}) \parallel \hat{n}$, consistent with the explicit closed-form $\vec{j}_1(v_{\text{host}}) = (6 r_0 / \phi^2)\hat{n}$ in F.1 v1.0 §7 — second-route confirmation of F.1 Theorem 7.1's structural component from symmetry alone, independent of the explicit first-order path-class enumeration. **Layer rigor**: publication-grade Layer 3 **unconditional** for the structural sub-question.
